# Skills · P · part 189

815 entries, principles to prior-authorization-ops. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **principles**: Engineering principles that bind any code Molcajete plans, builds, or reviews — integration tests as the trust contract, hexagonal default, dependency injection, 80% coverage floor, universal software craft (small functions, clear boundaries, no god files, refactor to reuse), technology-agnostic. Loaded by /m:plan and /m:execute. Mirrored to the host project at `.claude/rules/principles.md` by /m:setup. ([specd-sh/plugin](https://github.com/specd-sh/plugin/tree/HEAD/molcajete/shared/skills/principles) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **principles**: This skill should be used when the user is designing a module, refactoring, debugging, naming, decoupling, handling errors or concurrency, choosing inheritance vs composition, securing code, or asks "is this DRY", "what does The Pragmatic Programmer / Clean Code / Clean Architecture say about X", or "which principle applies here". Surfaces 1-4 relevant principles with the book, tip or chapter, and a pointer tied to the user's code. Advisory only; for a scan of a codebase use the review skill. ([w159/tech-tools](https://github.com/w159/tech-tools/tree/HEAD/plugins/programmer/skills/principles) · ★2)

- **prior-authorization-follow-up-list**: Prepare the referral desk's daily follow-up list from the coordinator's own spreadsheet of authorization requests waiting on a payer. The list says which requests are past the payer's turnaround with no call logged, which procedures are booked without an approval, which requests the payer is waiting on the practice for, which have no submission date, which are in the practice's system and missing from the list, and what could not be assessed. ([Fully-Powered-AI/healthcare-workflow-templates](https://github.com/Fully-Powered-AI/healthcare-workflow-templates/tree/HEAD/prior-authorization-follow-up-list))
- **prior-authorization-letter**: Write a persuasive prior-authorization / medical-necessity letter to an insurer. Use when asked to write a prior authorization letter, a letter of medical necessity, or to appeal a denied treatment/medication/procedure. Produces a structured letter — patient and request, clinical justification tied to guidelines, treatments tried, and the specific approval asked for — ready for clinician review and signature.
